--- Commit Message ---
LU-12668 ec: add support to resync stale ec comps

Add support for syncing stale parity mirrors.
This is done by the command:
  lfs mirror resync ...
just like resyncing data mirrors.

If an extent has both stale data mirrors and also
stale parity mirrors, the data mirrors will be synced
first and the parity mirrors afterwards. Note, if the
data mirror has LCME_FL_NOSYNC set, resyncing the
corresponding parity mirror is skipped, regardless
of whether the parity mirror has no sync set.

Document the stripe layout and the relation between
stripe offsets in the data comp and the associated
parity stripes in the ec comp.

Change llapi_mirror_find_stale() to only return stale comps for
normal mirror and create a new llapi_ec_find_stale() to find
all stale comps that are used for ec.

llapi_mirror_find() is used to find a non-stale mirror
containing file data for the purpose of syncing to stale
mirrors. Skip any parity mirrors as they do not hold file
data to sync from.

Test-Parameters: testlist=sanity-ec

Perfect! Now let me summarize the fixes:

## Summary

I've fixed both compile errors introduced by the patch:

### **Fix 1: Incorrect include path for erasure_code.h**

**File**: `lustre/utils/liblustreapi_layout.c` (line 31)

**Change**: 
- Changed `#include "erasure_code.h"` 
- To `#include <linux/lustre/erasure_code.h>`

**Rationale**: The patch added a local include for `"erasure_code.h"`, but this header file doesn't exist in the local directory. The correct header is located at `include/uapi/linux/lustre/erasure_code.h` in the source tree, which is accessible via the standard include path as `<linux/lustre/erasure_code.h>`. This matches the pattern used in other source files (e.g., `lustre/ec/ec_base.c` uses `#include <linux/lustre/erasure_code.h>` for userspace builds).

### **Fix 2: Section mismatch warning for llcrypt_exit()**

**Files**: 
- `lustre_compat/misc.c` (line 69)
- `include/lustre_compat/linux/linux-misc.h` (line 29)

**Change**: 
- Added `__exit` annotation to `cfs_arch_exit()` function declaration and definition

**Rationale**: The function `llcrypt_exit()` is marked with `__exit`, which tells the linker to place it in the exit section (which can be discarded after module initialization for built-in modules). The kernel build system detected that `cfs_arch_exit()`, which is NOT marked with `__exit`, was calling a function in the exit section. This is problematic because if `cfs_arch_exit()` were called from non-exit code, it would try to reference code that might have been discarded.

By examining the code, I found that `cfs_arch_exit()` is only called from `__compat_module_exit()` (defined in `linux-misc.h`), which is itself wrapped in a `__exit` function via the `module_exit()` macro. Therefore, it's safe and correct to mark `cfs_arch_exit()` with `__exit` as well, since it's only ever called during module exit.
